# The Star Dedication Registry -- Build Summary (Runs 1-4, complete)

An independent, premium symbolic star-dedication registry. This archive contains the complete
source across all four runs. The product is **feature-complete** and passes all local automated
gates; the only remaining work is live-services validation, a Lighthouse run, and a physical QR
scan (see `docs/PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`).

## Test status (all executed in the build environment)

|---|---|
| `scripts/test_allocation_logic.mjs` (Run 1) | 20 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_functions.mjs` (Run 2) | 36 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_qr.mjs` (QR) | 85 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_run3_functions.mjs` (Run 3) | 22 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_cert_pipeline.mjs` (server cert) | 8 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_e2e.mjs` (Run 4 end-to-end) | 17 passed, 0 failed |
| `scripts/test/test_artifacts.mjs` (real PDF + map) | 14 passed, 0 failed |
| Run A/B/C/D suites (keepsake, R2 PDF, experience, payments) | 21 + 15 + 28 + 19 = 83 passed, 0 failed |
| **Total** | **307 passed, 0 failed** (Runs 1-4: 202, artifacts 14 + cert 8 counted once; Runs A-D: 83) |

## Quality gates (Run 4, all clean)
- **CSP:** strict -- no `unsafe-inline` (styles externalised, inline JSON-LD hashed). Generated to
  `public/_headers` by `scripts/build_csp.mjs` (build fails if an inline style appears).
- **Accessibility:** 0 errors, 0 warnings (`scripts/a11y_audit.mjs`).
- **SEO:** 0 errors, 0 warnings (`scripts/seo_audit.mjs`).
- **Performance:** 0 errors, 0 warnings -- pages 15-28 KB (`scripts/perf_audit.mjs`).

Run everything: `npm run verify:all` (audits + tests).

## What each run delivered
- **Run 1 -- Foundation:** DB schema + RLS + public-safe views + atomic allocation/reservation +
  idempotent activation; HYG (CC BY-SA 4.0) import strategy (Gaia rejected as non-commercial;
  original viewer instead of Stellarium); premium static site + honesty disclosure. `RUN-1-REPORT`.
- **Run 2 -- Backend & flows:** Netlify Functions (reserve, draft, pay-init, signature-verified
  idempotent pay-webhook, verify, account, admin, scheduled expiry); Cashfree signature + amount
  checks; sanitisation, rate limiting, CORS. `RUN-2-REPORT`.
- **Run 3 -- Experience & artefacts:** from-scratch QR encoder (RS-verified), data-driven star map,
  premium PDF certificate, cinematic reveal (6 modes), interactive viewer, R2 signed media/
  downloads. `RUN-3-REPORT`.
- **Run 4 -- Hardening & launch-readiness:** strict CSP, all public pages (Section 19), a11y/SEO/perf
  audits, end-to-end test, and the full operations documentation set. `RUN-4-REPORT`.

## Honest status of live dependencies (verify before launch)
No Postgres/Supabase, network, Cashfree, or R2 in the build environment, so: migrations/RLS were
validated structurally + via a mock mirroring the RPC contract; Cashfree **signature algorithm**
ran for real (HTTP mocked); R2 **SigV4** is unit-tested (no live upload); the **QR** is RS-verified
+ decoder round-tripped but **not** phone-scanned; **Lighthouse** wasn't run (static perf-budget
audit used instead). All are enumerated in `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` and gated by
`docs/PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`.

FILE: docs\ADMIN-GUIDE.md

# Admin Guide -- The Star Dedication Registry

For registry administrators. All admin actions are gated by an email allow-list and every
mutation is written to the append-only audit trail (`registry_events`).

## Access
- Admin dashboard: `/admin/`.
- Sign in with a magic link to an email listed in `TSDR_ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS`.
- Non-allow-listed users get **403**. There is no password to leak.

## Dashboard
- **Metrics:** available/reserved/allocated stars, active/pending dedications, held/expired
  reservations, paid/failed payments, open content reports.

## Actions
### Content review (public/limited dedications)
- Enter the registration number, pick **approved / rejected / pending**, apply.
- Only `approved` (or `not_required`) public records appear in Explore.

### Status change (revoke / refund / reactivate)
- Enter the registration number, choose the status, **tick "Confirm destructive action"**, apply.
- Revoke/refund keep full history; the star stays allocated (a new dedication needs a new star).
- Every change writes a `status_changed` audit event with your email + reason.

### Corrections (via support workflow / API)
- Correctable fields: symbolic name, honouree, dedicator, message, privacy.
- The **allocated star and registration number cannot be changed** (immutability is enforced;
  attempts return `FIELD_NOT_CORRECTABLE`). Reissue a certificate after correcting.

### Certificate reissue
- Trigger the internal `generate-certificate` function (server-side, `X-TSDR-Internal` secret)
  with `reason:"correction"|"reissue"`. A new `certificate_versions` row is created.

## What admins must NOT do
- There is **no unaudited hard-delete** for active dedications -- by design. Use revoke instead.
- Never paste secrets into the browser or share your magic-link.
- Don't approve public content that exposes third-party personal data without consent
  (see `CONTENT-POLICY`).

## Auditing
- Every admin action -> `registry_events` (actor_type `admin`, your email, before/after, reason).
- Review the audit trail for any dedication when handling disputes.

## Escalation
- Payment anomalies -> Operations Manual -> "Payments succeed but don't activate".
- Suspected data exposure -> Operations Manual -> "Private data exposure report".

FILE: docs\ARCHITECTURE.md

# Architecture -- The Star Dedication Registry

## Guiding constraints (from the handover)
Static/lightweight frontend \u00b7 Netlify hosting + Functions \u00b7 Supabase (DB + Auth) \u00b7
Cloudflare R2 (durable artefacts) \u00b7 Cashfree-compatible payments \u00b7 transactional email via
env-configured provider \u00b7 **no secrets in the browser** \u00b7 cost-conscious (few deploys,
event-driven not polling) \u00b7 original viewer (no heavy framework "just for effects").

## Stack decision (and justification)
- **No SPA framework.** The public site is static HTML + one small vanilla JS file. This
  keeps Core Web Vitals excellent on budget Android, avoids a bundler/deploy tax, and matches
  your existing static-frontend operating model. A framework would add weight without a
  proportional benefit for mostly content + a few interactive flows.
- **Netlify Functions** for every privileged action (reserve, draft, activate, verify,
  certificate/map generation, admin, integration). They alone hold the service-role key and
  provider secrets.
- **Supabase Postgres** is the **source of truth** for allocation, registrations, audit.
- **Cloudflare R2** holds generated certificates, star maps and uploaded media; access via
  **signed URLs** only (private artefacts never public).
- **Interactive sky viewer (Run 3):** original Canvas/WebGL renderer over licensed HYG
  coordinates -- no Stellarium embedding (licence + maintainer stance; see DATASET doc).

## Component map
```
Browser (static)                         Netlify Functions (trusted)         Supabase
-----------------                        ---------------------------         ---------
index/dedicate/verify/explore  -- fetch -> /reserve   - RPC -> tsdr_reserve_star()  +
account/ (Supabase auth JS)               /draft     - RPC -> tsdr_create_draft_... | RLS +
admin/ (gated)                            /pay/init  ---------> Cashfree           | SECURITY
                                          /pay/webhook -verify-> tsdr_activate_...    | DEFINER
                                          /verify    - view --> v_verification      | funcs
                                          /cert /map ---------> R2 (signed)         +
       ^                                        |
       +------------ signed URLs ---------------+
```

## Data flow: create a dedication (Journey A)
1. `POST /reserve` -> `tsdr_reserve_star()` atomically holds one eligible star, returns
   `{reservation_id, raw token, star facts, expires_at}`. Token hash stored server-side.
2. `POST /draft` with the raw token -> `tsdr_create_draft_dedication()` (token hash verified,
   hold live) -> `dedication` in `pending_payment`.
3. `POST /pay/init` -> Cashfree order; client completes payment.
4. Cashfree **webhook** -> function verifies **signature + amount/currency** ->
   `tsdr_activate_paid_dedication(idempotency_key)` assigns the registration number, allocates
   the star, consumes the reservation. Idempotent against duplicate/out-of-order webhooks.
5. Post-activation jobs generate certificate + star map to R2 and mark `reveal_status=ready`.

## Environments
- **Preview/sandbox:** `CASHFREE_MODE=sandbox`, demo seed pool, verification endpoint live.
- **Production:** real HYG seed, production Cashfree, PITR backups on.

## Cost/deploy discipline
- Static assets cached; functions are event-driven. Reservation expiry runs as a **scheduled**
  function (cron) rather than client polling. Bundle related changes per deploy.

## Directory layout
```
public/                 static site (served by Netlify)
  index.html, verify.html, 404.html, robots.txt, sitemap.xml
  css/tsdr.css   js/tsdr.js   legal/...   assets/...
netlify/functions/      privileged endpoints (Run 2+)
  _shared/              supabase client, auth, sanitise, cashfree, r2, rate-limit helpers
supabase/migrations/    0001 schema \u00b7 0002 RLS \u00b7 0003 views \u00b7 0004 functions \u00b7
                        0005 demo seed (generated) \u00b7 0006 real HYG seed (generated on your box)
scripts/                import_hyg.mjs \u00b7 generate_demo_pool.mjs \u00b7 test_allocation_logic.mjs
docs/                   this file + reports, security, dataset, manifest, integration contract
```

## Migration order (apply top-to-bottom)
`0001 -> 0002 -> 0003 -> 0004 -> (0005 demo OR 0006 real HYG)`. 0003 views depend on 0001 tables;
0004 functions depend on 0001; 0002 must run before exposing anything. Seeds run last.

## Future ZivoGift integration
Kept out of this run by design; a signed server-to-server contract is specified in
`docs/ZIVOGIFT-INTEGRATION-CONTRACT.md`. The Registry remains source of truth for allocation,
registration number, activation, certificate, verification and status.

FILE: docs\BACKUPS-AND-DR.md
=====================================================
# Backups & Disaster Recovery -- The Star Dedication Registry

The registry is the source of truth for star allocation, registrations, certificates metadata,
verification and audit. Losing it is the worst-case scenario, so backups are defined and must be
**tested**, not assumed.

## What must be backed up
| Asset | Store | Backup mechanism | RPO target | RTO target |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Registry database | Supabase Postgres | **PITR** + daily logical dump | <= 5 min (PITR) | <= 1 hr |
| Generated artefacts (PDF/PNG) | Cloudflare R2 | Versioning + lifecycle; optional cross-bucket copy | <= 24 hr | <= 1 hr |
| Uploaded media | Cloudflare R2 | Versioning | <= 24 hr | <= 1 hr |
| Config/secrets | Netlify env | Documented in `.env.example`; secrets in a password manager | n/a | <= 15 min |
| Source code | Git | Remote (GitHub) | commit | <= 15 min |

> Artefacts are **reproducible** from the DB via `generate-certificate`, so the database is the
> critical asset. If R2 is lost, certificates/maps can be regenerated for every active record.

## Database backups
1. **Enable PITR** in Supabase (paid tier). Confirm the retention window (e.g. 7 days).
2. **Daily logical dump** (defence in depth), stored off-Supabase:
   ```bash
   pg_dump "$SUPABASE_DB_URL" --no-owner --format=custom > tsdr_$(date +%F).dump
   # upload the dump to R2 or another provider; keep 30 days
   ```
3. Store the dump encrypted; restrict access.

## Restore procedures (tested quarterly)
### Point-in-time restore (accidental bad migration/data loss)
1. In Supabase, restore to a timestamp just before the incident.
2. Re-point the app (URL/keys unchanged if in-place; otherwise update Netlify env).
3. Run the smoke test + a subset of `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md`.

### Logical restore (new project)
1. Create a fresh Supabase project; apply migrations `0001-0010`.
2. `pg_restore --no-owner -d "$NEW_DB_URL" tsdr_YYYY-MM-DD.dump`.
3. Update Netlify env to the new project; redeploy; smoke test.

### Artefact regeneration (R2 loss)
1. For each active dedication, call `generate-certificate` (internal secret). Certificates and
   star maps are re-created from the DB and re-uploaded. Verification QR still resolves (it points
   at the DB-backed verify URL).

## DR runbook (region/provider outage)
- **Netlify down:** static site + functions unavailable. Communicate status; no data loss.
  Optionally keep a secondary host on standby for the static site.
- **Supabase down:** reads/writes fail. Wait for recovery or restore logical dump to a new
  project and repoint. Activation is idempotent, so replayed webhooks won't double-charge state.
- **Cashfree down:** new payments fail; existing records unaffected. Webhooks will be redelivered.

## Verification of backups (do not skip)
- **Quarterly:** perform a logical restore into a scratch project and run the test suite +
  `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` Section 1 (DB) to prove the dump is usable.
- **After any schema change:** take a fresh dump and confirm it restores cleanly.

## Retention
- PITR: provider window (>= 7 days). Daily dumps: 30 days. Artefact versions: 90 days.
- Never hard-delete issued registrations; retain audit history indefinitely.

FILE: docs\CERTIFICATE-AND-MEDIA.md

# Certificate, Star Map, QR & Media -- Generation Guide (Run 3)

## Pipeline overview
```
activation -> generate-certificate (internal, secret-gated)
                +- tsdr_cert_source(dedication)          -> record + star fields
                +- tsdr_star_neighbors(ra,dec,fov)        -> viewer/map context
                +- buildStarMapSVG -> sharp PNG -> R2 (starmaps/REG.png)
                +- encodeQR(verifyUrl) -> qrToSvg          -> embedded in PDF
                +- generateCertificate(pdf-lib)           -> certificates/REG.pdf -> R2
                +- tsdr_record_certificate(hash)          -> certificate_versions++
                +- tsdr_attach_starmap(key)               -> dedications.star_map_object_key
downloads -> download (signed R2 GET, 5-min TTL; token required for private)
```

## QR (lib/qr.mjs)
- Byte mode, ECC **M** by default, versions 1-10, all masks, RS over GF(256).
- Verified here via RS syndromes = 0 **and** an internal decoder round-trip
  (`lib/qr_decode.mjs`, `scripts/test/test_qr.mjs`). **QA must still scan on a phone.**
- `qrToSvg()` -> SVG; rasterised to PNG by `sharp` for embedding.
- The QR resolves to `${TSDR_PUBLIC_BASE_URL}/verify.html?rn=REG` -- the DB record is the source
  of truth; the PDF is only a rendering.

## Star map (lib/starmap.mjs)
- Gnomonic (tangent-plane) projection about the allocated star; centre star lands at chart
  centre (asserted in tests). Dots scaled by apparent magnitude.
- Clearly labelled **"artistic rendering of real J2000 positions -- not a photograph"** and
  carries **HYG (CC BY-SA 4.0)** attribution. Never call it a telescope photo.
- Outputs SVG (interactive/print) and PNG (via sharp) for the certificate.

## Certificate (lib/certificate.mjs)
- pdf-lib A4 portrait. Includes: seal, symbolic name, honouree, message, occasion, date,
  constellation, **J2000 coordinates**, **scientific identifier**, registration number, QR,
  issue date, **authenticity hash** (sha-256 of key fields), and the **symbolic-dedication
  disclosure** at readable size.
- **WinAnsi safety:** standard fonts can't encode some Unicode (e.g. U+2212 minus, curly
  quotes, em-dash); `safe()` maps these to Latin-1 equivalents so any user text renders.
  (A future embedded TrueType via fontkit can restore full Unicode + custom typography.)
- No fabricated human signature -- role is **"Registry Administrator"**.
- Formats: digital PDF + print-quality A4 today; landscape/mobile-share are trivial size
  variants to add in Run 4.

## Media (netlify/functions/media-upload.mjs + _shared/r2.mjs)
- Owner-checked (`tsdr_media_register`), **type + size validated** (image <=8 MB jpeg/png/webp;
  audio <=20 MB; video <=80 MB), then a **signed PUT** lets the browser upload straight to R2.
- Object keys are namespaced `media/<dedication>/<random>`; media rows start `pending`
  (scan/approve workflow can flip to `ready`).
- Served later only via signed GET (never public).

## R2 signing (_shared/r2.mjs, _shared/r2put.mjs)
- AWS SigV4, region `auto`, pure Node crypto. `presignR2` for query-signed GET/PUT links;
  `putObjectR2` for server-side authenticated PUT with a payload hash.
- Tested for structure/determinism; **confirm against a live bucket** in Run 4.

## Reveal (public/reveal.html + js/reveal.js)
- Sound **off by default** (WebAudio tone created only on tap), **Skip** control, reduced-motion
  path, accessible live-text summary. Six modes; memorial restrained (no upsell).
- Data via `window.TSDR_REVEAL` (demo) or `/.netlify/functions/reveal` (prod). Private/unlisted
  require the token in the link (`?t=`); public can use `?rn=`.

## Operational notes
- `generate-certificate` is **internal** (`X-TSDR-Internal: $TSDR_INTERNAL_SECRET`); call it from
  an activation trigger or admin action, never from the browser.
- Certificate reissue: call again with `reason:"reissue"|"correction"` -> new
  `certificate_versions` row, keys updated, event audited.

# Astronomical Data & Licensing -- TSDR

This document records provenance and licence terms for every dataset and third-party
component considered for The Star Dedication Registry. It is the source of the public
`/legal/data-sources.html` page and satisfies the "document before adopting a dataset" rule.

## Decision summary

| Source | Licence | Commercial use? | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HYG Database v4.4** (Hipparcos + Yale Bright Star + Gliese) | **CC BY-SA 4.0** | [x] Yes, with attribution + share-alike on the derived dataset | **ADOPTED** as the primary catalogue |
| **Gaia** (DR3/DR4) | **CC BY-NC 3.0 IGO** |  **Non-commercial only** | **REJECTED** for this commercial registry |
| **Stellarium** (desktop) | **GPL v2** | ! Copyleft; would force source disclosure of derivatives | Not embedded |
| **Stellarium Web Engine** | GPL v2 + separate commercial licensing enquiry required; maintainers explicitly discourage star-registry use | ! | Not used -- we build an **original viewer** on HYG data |

### Why HYG
- HYG is a curated, human-scale catalogue (~119,614 stars) combining Hipparcos, the Yale
  Bright Star Catalog, and the Gliese nearby-star catalog -- ideal for a *premium, curated*
  allocation pool rather than a billion-row dump.
- Licence: **Creative Commons Attribution-ShareAlike 4.0** (v4.x). Commercial use is
  permitted provided we (a) attribute, and (b) license our **derived dataset** under the
  same CC BY-SA terms. This share-alike attaches to the *database/derived data*, not to our
  application source code, which remains our own.
- Source of truth: `https://codeberg.org/astronexus/hyg` (also mirrored at astronexus.com).

### Required attribution (must appear on-site and in generated seed files)
> Star data derived from the **HYG Database v4.4** (c) David Nash / astronexus,
> licensed under **CC BY-SA 4.0**. https://codeberg.org/astronexus/hyg

This string is embedded in:
- the site footer and `/legal/data-sources.html`,
- the header of every generated seed SQL file (see `scripts/import_hyg.mjs`),
- `package.json` `dataAttribution`.

### Why NOT Gaia
Gaia data is distributed under **CC BY-NC 3.0 IGO** -- the **NC (non-commercial)** clause
makes it unsuitable for a paid product without a separate commercial agreement with ESA.
We therefore do not import or ship Gaia data.

### Why an original viewer instead of Stellarium
Stellarium (desktop) is GPLv2; the Stellarium **Web Engine** requires a direct commercial
licensing conversation, and the maintainers have publicly stated they discourage
star-registry businesses. To keep TSDR proprietary, dependency-light and unambiguous, the
interactive sky viewer (Run 3) will be an **original** Canvas/WebGL renderer plotting HYG
coordinates we already licence. If a compliant third-party viewer is later integrated, it
will be labelled and licensed correctly.

## Imported fields (from HYG -> `star_catalog`)
| HYG field | TSDR column | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `ra` (hours) | `right_ascension` (degrees) | converted x15, normalised to [0,360) |
| `dec` | `declination` | degrees, validated [-90,90] |
| `mag` | `apparent_magnitude` | drives `visibility_class` |
| `con` | `constellation` | IAU 3-letter abbreviation |
| `ci` | `color_index` | B-V, if present |
| `dist` | `distance_parsecs` | dropped if >= 100000 (dubious parallax) |
| `hip`/`hd`/`hr` | `source_identifier` / `additional_metadata` | preference HIP > HD > HR |
| `spect` | `additional_metadata.spect` | spectral type |

## Curation rules (premium pool, not the whole sky)
Configured in `scripts/import_hyg.mjs`:
- valid finite RA/Dec/magnitude only;
- magnitude cutoff (`--max-mag`, default **6.5** ~= naked-eye limit for launch pool);
- exclude dubious distance (`dist >= 100000`);
- exclude IAU **proper-named** famous stars (avoids "you named Sirius" confusion);
- balance per-constellation coverage (`--per-constellation`, default 400);
- overall `--limit` (default 20,000) for a curated, premium pool.

## Visibility classification (honest, magnitude-based)
`naked_eye <= 6.0 < binocular <= 9.0 < small_telescope <= 12.0 < telescope`.
No naked-eye promise is made for a star fainter than the naked-eye threshold, and the
certificate/viewer must state when equipment is required.

## Update strategy
- Pin the HYG release string (`HYG v4.4 (2025)`) in `source_release`.
- Re-running the importer is idempotent (`on conflict (source_catalog, source_identifier)
  do nothing`), so catalogue refreshes never duplicate or silently re-allocate stars.
- Any allocated star is never removed by a re-import; allocation state lives in TSDR, not HYG.

## Demo (synthetic) data -- clearly separated
For pre-launch/testing only, `scripts/generate_demo_pool.mjs` emits rows stamped
`source_catalog = 'TSDR-DEMO'`, `source_release = 'SYNTHETIC -- NOT A REAL CATALOGUE'`, and
`additional_metadata.demo = true`. These must be removed/replaced by the real HYG seed
before launch. Explore/demo records are visibly labelled in the UI.

=====================================================
# Deployment Guide -- The Star Dedication Registry

End-to-end steps to deploy TSDR to production on Netlify + Supabase + Cloudflare R2 +
Cashfree. Follow in order. Nothing here requires editing source.

## 0. Prerequisites
- A domain (e.g. `stardedicationregistry.com`).
- Accounts: Netlify, Supabase, Cloudflare (R2), Cashfree, a transactional email provider.
- Node 18+ locally to run the test/audit suite before shipping.

## 1. Supabase
1. Create a project; note `Project URL`, `anon` key, `service_role` key.
2. In the SQL editor, run migrations **in order**:
   `0001 -> 0002 -> 0003 -> 0004 -> 0007 -> 0008 -> 0009 -> 0010`.
3. Seed the catalogue:
   - **Launch data:** on a networked machine, download HYG v4.4 CSV, then
     `npm run import:hyg` -> apply the generated `0006_seed_catalog.sql`.
   - (Dev only) `npm run seed:demo` -> apply `0005_seed_demo_pool.sql`.
4. Enable **PITR backups** (see `BACKUPS-AND-DR.md`).
5. Auth: enable email magic-link; set the Site URL + redirect URLs to your domain.

## 2. Cloudflare R2
1. Create a bucket, e.g. `tsdr-artifacts` (private).
2. Create an API token (Access Key ID + Secret) scoped to that bucket.
3. Note your R2 **Account ID**. No public bucket access -- all reads are signed.

## 3. Cashfree
1. Start in **sandbox** (`CASHFREE_MODE=sandbox`). Note App ID + Secret.
2. Set the webhook URL to `https://<your-domain>/.netlify/functions/pay-webhook`.
3. Confirm the webhook uses your PG **client secret** for signatures (that's the key we verify with).

## 4. Netlify
1. Connect the repo. Build settings come from `netlify.toml`:
   - Build command: `node scripts/build_csp.mjs` (generates the strict CSP `_headers`).
   - Publish dir: `public`; Functions dir: `netlify/functions`.
2. Set environment variables (from `.env.example`) in **Site settings -> Environment**:
   - `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`
   - `CASHFREE_APP_ID`, `CASHFREE_SECRET_KEY`, `CASHFREE_MODE`
   - `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `R2_BUCKET`
   - `EMAIL_PROVIDER`, `EMAIL_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM`
   - `TSDR_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` (your https domain), `TSDR_RESERVATION_MINUTES`
   - `TSDR_ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS`, `TSDR_INTERNAL_SECRET`
3. Publish the front-end config for auth pages: set `window.TSDR_SUPABASE_URL` and
   `window.TSDR_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (e.g. via a small `/js/config.js` you add to `public/`,
   referenced before `account.js`/`admin.js`). These are the public, RLS-protected values.
4. Deploy. The scheduled `expire-reservations` function runs every 10 minutes automatically.

## 5. Verify the deploy (smoke test)
- Load `/` -> hero + starfield render; Lighthouse mobile >= 90 expected (pages are 15-28 KB).
- `/.netlify/functions/verify?rn=TSDR-2026-XXXXXXXX` -> 400/404 as expected.
- Run the **live** integration pass in `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` (reserve -> pay -> webhook ->
  verify -> reveal -> certificate -> download) in **sandbox** first.
- Confirm response headers contain the strict CSP (from `public/_headers`).

## 6. Go-live
- Complete `PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`.
- Switch `CASHFREE_MODE=production` and production keys.
- Replace demo seed with the real HYG seed; remove any `TSDR-DEMO` rows.
- Announce.

## Rollback
- **Front-end/functions:** redeploy the previous Netlify deploy (one click) or `git revert`.
- **Database:** see per-migration rollback in each `FILES-MANIFEST*.md`; restore from PITR if
  needed (`BACKUPS-AND-DR.md`). Issued registrations are never hard-deleted.

## Cost notes (you are cost-conscious)
- Static pages are cached immutably (`_headers`); functions are event-driven; reservation
  expiry is a cron, not client polling. Bundle changes to avoid repeated deploys.

All paths relative to project root. **A = added** this run. (Nothing modified/removed -- clean start.)

**Directories reserved for later runs:** `netlify/functions/_shared/` (Run 2 helpers),
`public/assets/` (OG image, fonts).

## Rollback (Run 1)
Run 1 adds files only; nothing to roll back in an existing system. If migrations were applied
to a database, reverse in order: drop functions (0004), drop views (0003), drop policies +
`disable row level security` (0002), `drop table` in dependency order (0001). Seed data
(`0005`) is removed with `delete from star_catalog where source_catalog='TSDR-DEMO';`.

FILE: docs\INTEGRATION-TESTING.md

# Integration Testing -- Run 2 (live Supabase + Cashfree sandbox)

The handler tests (`npm run test:functions`) prove logic against a mock. This guide is the
**live** pass you run once, with real credentials, before launch. It maps 1:1 to the handover's
Run-2 test list (Section 24).

## 0. Prerequisites
- Supabase project created; `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`.
- Cashfree **sandbox** app id + secret; `CASHFREE_MODE=sandbox`.
- Netlify site (or `netlify dev`) with env vars set; a public URL for the webhook.
- Migrations applied in order: `0001 -> 0002 -> 0003 -> 0004 -> 0007 -> 0008`, then a seed
  (`0005` demo for testing).

## 1. Database (run in Supabase SQL editor)
```sql
-- Atomic allocation: two sessions cannot grab the same star.
select * from tsdr_reserve_star('itest-A', null, null, 20);
select * from tsdr_reserve_star('itest-B', null, null, 20);   -- different star_id

-- Reservation expiry frees the star.
update star_reservations set expires_at = now() - interval '1 min' where status='held';
select tsdr_expire_reservations();                              -- > 0; star back to 'available'

-- One active dedication per star (partial unique index) -- expect failure on 2nd active.
-- RLS: as anon (anon key), selecting star_catalog/dedications returns 0 rows / denied.
select count(*) from v_public_dedications;                      -- only public+approved
```
Confirm: unique reg-number format, `registry_events` rows appended, public views hide PII.

## 2. Payment (Cashfree sandbox + webhook)
1. `POST /reserve` -> `POST /draft` -> `POST /pay-init` -> open the returned `payment_session_id`
   in the sandbox checkout; complete a **success** test payment.
2. Verify the webhook activated exactly one dedication and returned a registration number.
3. **Duplicate webhook:** re-POST the same payload -> `already_processed:true`, same number.
4. **Out-of-order / failed:** send a FAILED event -> acknowledged, not activated.
5. **Invalid signature:** tamper the body -> `401 TSDR_SIGNATURE_INVALID`.
6. **Amount/currency mismatch:** alter `order_amount`/`order_currency` (re-sign) ->
   `400 TSDR_AMOUNT_MISMATCH` / `TSDR_CURRENCY_MISMATCH`.

> Signature to reproduce a valid test event:
> `sig = base64(hmac_sha256(secret=CASHFREE_SECRET_KEY, msg = x-webhook-timestamp + rawBody))`.

## 3. Verification
- Valid public / valid private / invalid / revoked numbers -> statuses correct; private/invalid
  are **indistinguishable** (uniform 404). Hammer >15/5min -> `429`. Case/format variants
  normalise. Confirm **no names/message** in any response.

## 4. Customer account
- Magic-link sign in; `GET /account` with the JWT lists only your rows. Try another user's JWT ->
  you never see foreign dedications (RLS + verified-id).

## 5. Admin
- Non-admin JWT -> `403`. Admin metrics -> `200`. `set_status` without `confirm` -> `400`; with
  `confirm` -> `200` and a `registry_events` row. Attempt to "correct" `star_catalog_id` -> `400`.

## 6. Security spot-checks
- IDOR: call `/draft` with a valid `reservation_id` but wrong token -> `400`.
- XSS: submit `<script>` in names -> stored escaped; rendered safely on account/verify.
- CORS: request from a non-allowed origin -> `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: null`.
- Service role never present in any browser bundle (grep the deployed `public/` + client JS).

## 7. Sign-off
Record pass/fail for each item above in the launch checklist. Only proceed to Run 3 once the
payment + verification + admin flows pass against live sandbox services.

FILE: docs\KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md
=====================================================
# Known Limitations -- The Star Dedication Registry

Honest, current limitations as of the end of Run 4. None are hidden; each has a mitigation or a
clear owner for launch.

## Verified by mock/logic, not yet by live services
- The **database migrations, RLS, and RPCs** were validated structurally and via a mock that
  mirrors the RPC contract -- **not** yet executed against a live Supabase project. Do the live
  pass in `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` Section 1.
- **Cashfree**: only the signature algorithm ran for real; order creation + webhook delivery are
  mocked in tests. Run the sandbox pass (Section 2) then a real production transaction.
- **Cloudflare R2**: SigV4 signing is unit-tested for structure/determinism; **no live upload/
  download** has occurred. Confirm a real round-trip.

## QR code
- The encoder is **spec-correct and self-verified** (Reed-Solomon syndromes = 0; internal decoder
  round-trips every test URL; tamper flips syndromes). It has **not been scanned by a physical
  phone** in this environment. Print + scan on iOS/Android before launch (pre-launch checklist).

## Star map
- With the synthetic demo pool, maps look sparse (few catalogue stars fall within the field of
  view). The **real HYG import** makes them dense. Projection maths is validated regardless.
- The map is an **artistic rendering** of real positions, clearly labelled -- not a photograph.

## Certificate typography
- pdf-lib standard fonts are **WinAnsi**; a `safe()` sanitiser maps non-Latin-1 glyphs (curly
  quotes, em-dash, minus, ellipsis) to safe equivalents so any input renders without crashing.
  **Non-Latin scripts** (e.g. Devanagari, CJK) are stripped in the PDF today. To support them,
  embed a Unicode TrueType via fontkit in a follow-up (the data/record already stores full
  Unicode; only the PDF rendering is limited).

## Reveal audio
- Ambient sound is a gentle WebAudio tone generated on user tap (no external asset), off by
  default. If you want richer, mode-specific music, add licensed audio files later.

## CSP
- `script-src` uses **hashes** for inline JSON-LD (not per-request nonces), which is ideal for a
  static host and requires no edge function. If you later add many inline scripts, consider an
  edge nonce approach. Cashfree SDK + jsDelivr (Supabase) are the only external script hosts.

## Content moderation
- Public/limited dedications go through an **admin review** state, but there is no automated
  text/image moderation. For scale, add an automated filter feeding the review queue.

## Email
- Transactional email is provider-agnostic via env vars but the actual templates/sends are wired
  at deploy time; verify deliverability (SPF/DKIM) for your domain.

## Internationalisation
- UI copy is English only. Structure supports adding locales later; not in scope yet.

## Analytics
- No analytics/telemetry is bundled (privacy-friendly by default). Add a privacy-respecting
  analytics tool if you want conversion insight; update the CSP `connect-src`/`script-src`.

## ZivoGift integration
- Intentionally **not** implemented; a signed server-to-server contract is designed in
  `ZIVOGIFT-INTEGRATION-CONTRACT.md` for a later run.

FILE: docs\OPERATIONS-MANUAL.md
=====================================================
# Operations Manual -- The Star Dedication Registry

Day-to-day running of TSDR: routine tasks, monitoring, and incident response.

## Architecture recap
Static site (Netlify) -> Netlify Functions (trusted) -> Supabase Postgres (source of truth) ->
Cloudflare R2 (artefacts) + Cashfree (payments) + email provider. Secrets live only in
Netlify env / server functions. See `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## Routine tasks
| Task | How | Frequency |
|---|---|---|
| Reservation expiry | Automatic (`expire-reservations` cron, every 10 min) | continuous |
| Catalogue availability | Admin dashboard -> metrics (`stars_available`) | weekly |
| Content review queue | Admin dashboard -> review public/limited dedications | daily |
| Content reports | Admin -> resolve `content_reports` | daily |
| Payment exceptions | Admin metrics (`payments_failed`) + Cashfree dashboard | daily |
| Catalogue top-up | Re-run `import:hyg` with higher `--limit` when `stars_available` low | as needed |
| Certificate reissues | Trigger `generate-certificate` with `reason:"reissue"` | as needed |

## Catalogue top-up (avoid running out of stars)
1. On a networked box: download the latest HYG CSV.
2. `node scripts/import_hyg.mjs --in hyg_v44.csv --out supabase/migrations/00XX_seed_catalog.sql --limit 40000`.
3. Apply the new seed SQL (idempotent -- `on conflict do nothing`, so no duplicates/re-allocation).
4. Confirm `stars_available` rose in admin metrics.

## Monitoring & alerts (recommended)
- **Netlify:** function error rate + invocation counts (watch `pay-webhook`, `generate-certificate`).
- **Supabase:** DB CPU, connection count, error logs; alert on failed RPCs.
- **Cashfree:** settlement + webhook delivery dashboard.
- **Uptime:** external monitor on `/` and `/.netlify/functions/verify?rn=TSDR-2026-TESTTEST` (expects 400/404).
- **R2:** storage growth + 4xx on signed URLs.

## Incident playbooks
### Payments succeed but dedications don't activate
1. Check `pay-webhook` logs for `SIGNATURE_INVALID` (wrong `CASHFREE_SECRET_KEY`) or
   `AMOUNT_MISMATCH`.
2. Verify the webhook URL + that Cashfree signs with the client secret.
3. Re-drive: Cashfree can resend the webhook; activation is **idempotent**, so replays are safe.
4. If needed, admin can confirm the `payments` row and the `dedications.registration_status`.

### "No star available"
- `stars_available` is low or a constellation filter is too narrow -> run a catalogue top-up, or
  advise "Registry-selected (any)".

### Certificate/star-map missing
- Re-invoke `generate-certificate` (internal secret) for the dedication id. It's safe to re-run;
  a new `certificate_versions` row is added and keys updated.

### Suspected abuse / enumeration
- Verify + reveal + download are rate-limited (`rate_limits` table). Check for many `429`s from
  one IP; tighten limits in the function if sustained.

### Private data exposure report
- Confirm the record's `privacy_level`. Public reads come only from `v_public_dedications`;
  private/unlisted require a token. If a public record shouldn't be public, admin -> `set_status`
  or `review: rejected` to remove it from Explore; then investigate.

## Deploys
- Prefer bundling changes (cost-conscious). Every deploy re-runs `build_csp.mjs`; if an inline
  style/script slipped in, the build **fails loudly** -- fix and redeploy.
- Roll back via Netlify "Published deploys" (one click).

## Data lifecycle
- Issued registrations are **never hard-deleted**. Revoked/refunded keep history + audit events.
- Media is private in R2; served via short-lived signed URLs only.

FILE: docs\RUN-1-REPORT.md

# Run 1 Report -- The Star Dedication Registry

**Scope of Run 1 (per Section 27):** audit supplied files \u00b7 confirm architecture \u00b7 foundational
public site \u00b7 database design + migrations \u00b7 catalogue import strategy \u00b7 allocation &
reservation foundations \u00b7 security baseline. *No ZIP generated (per Section 25/Section 27).*

---

## What changed (this run)
Created the TSDR project from scratch (no starter files were supplied in this chat -- uploads
and workspace were empty, so Run 1 begins clean).

**Database (Supabase/Postgres)**
- `0001_core_schema.sql` -- 9 tables: `star_catalog`, `star_reservations`, `dedications`,
  `dedication_media`, `registry_events` (append-only), `payments`, `certificate_versions`,
  `content_reports`, `ref_occasions`; UUID PKs, CHECK constraints, partial-unique indexes,
  `updated_at` triggers.
- `0002_rls_policies.sql` -- RLS **enabled + forced** on all sensitive tables; deny-by-default
  for `anon`; owner-scoped read/update for authenticated users; report-insert only.
- `0003_public_views.sql` -- public-safe views (`v_public_dedications`, `v_verification`,
  `v_catalog_availability`) exposing only whitelisted columns.
- `0004_allocation_functions.sql` -- 5 `SECURITY DEFINER` functions: atomic
  `tsdr_reserve_star` (FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED), `tsdr_expire_reservations`,
  `tsdr_create_draft_dedication` (token-hash verified), idempotent
  `tsdr_activate_paid_dedication`, and secure `tsdr_gen_registration_number`; EXECUTE revoked
  from anon/authenticated.

**Catalogue import strategy**
- `scripts/import_hyg.mjs` -- production importer (HYG v4.4 -> curated seed SQL, with
  attribution baked in). Runs on a networked machine against a downloaded HYG CSV.
- `scripts/generate_demo_pool.mjs` -- synthetic **TSDR-DEMO** seed for pre-launch testing
  (clearly labelled, never real data).
- `0005_seed_demo_pool.sql` -- generated demo seed (300 stars).

**Public site (static, mobile-first, accessible)**
- Design system `css/tsdr.css` + `js/tsdr.js` (reduced-motion starfield, no autoplay,
  verification form wired to the Run 2 endpoint).
- `index.html` (premium homepage with honest disclosure front-and-centre, occasions, registry
  standards, data transparency, SEO + Organization JSON-LD), `legal/disclosure.html` (full
  honesty page + FAQ JSON-LD), `verify.html`, `404.html`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`.

**Config & docs**
- `netlify.toml` (security headers + CSP + noindex for private routes), `.env.example`
  (no secrets), `.gitignore`, `package.json`.
- `docs/`: ARCHITECTURE, SECURITY-BASELINE, DATASET-AND-LICENSING, ZIVOGIFT-INTEGRATION-
  CONTRACT, this report, and FILES-MANIFEST.

## Why it changed
Establishes a correct, secure, honest foundation so Runs 2-4 add behaviour without reworking
the core. Key decisions: **HYG (CC BY-SA 4.0)** as the catalogue; **reject Gaia (non-commercial)**;
**original viewer** instead of Stellarium (GPL + maintainer stance); **static frontend** to
protect Core Web Vitals and minimise deploys/cost.

## What was actually RUN (in this environment)
```
$ node scripts/generate_demo_pool.mjs --count 300
  -> generated 300, validationErrors 0, constellationsCovered 83,
    visibility {naked_eye:136, binocular:95, small_telescope:69},
    RAin[1.438,359.823], Decin[-79.194,86.724]

$ node scripts/test_allocation_logic.mjs
  -> RESULT: 20 passed, 0 failed
```
Structural SQL checks (grep/node): 9 tables, 9 RLS-enable statements, 5 functions,
300 demo value-rows, begin/commit balanced, all demo coordinates within valid ranges.

## What was actually TESTED (and how)
- **Design/logic tests (executed, passing):** atomic single-winner allocation under 20
  concurrent attempts on a 5-star pool; reservation expiry frees & re-allocates; draft
  creation rejects wrong reservation token (IDOR); **idempotent** activation across
  duplicate/out-of-order webhooks returns one stable registration number and exactly one
  active dedication per star; 20,000 registration numbers unique + correct format +
  non-sequential; coordinate-range validation.
  These use an **in-memory model that mirrors the SQL** (see honesty note below).
- **Structural validation (executed):** SQL object counts, seed row counts, begin/commit
  balance, demo-JSON coordinate validity.

## HONEST limitations -- what was NOT executed here

# Run 2 Report -- The Star Dedication Registry

**Scope (per Section 27 Run 2):** registration creation \u00b7 payment-ready activation \u00b7 verification \u00b7
customer authentication & account \u00b7 admin operations \u00b7 audit trail. Plus the Netlify Function
layer, Cashfree signature/amount checks, sanitisation, rate limiting, CORS/CSRF posture, and a
real handler-level test run. *No ZIP generated (per Section 25/Section 27).*

---

## What changed (this run)

### Netlify Functions (`netlify/functions/`)
Thin `handler` entrypoints delegate to a testable `core(event, deps)` (dependency injection).
- `reserve.mjs` -- atomic star hold via `tsdr_reserve_star`; rate-limited; returns a one-time
  reservation token.
- `draft.mjs` -- creates a draft dedication bound to a valid held reservation (token-hash
  verified); validates + sanitises input.
- `pay-init.mjs` -- **server-authoritative price**; creates a Cashfree order and returns
  `payment_session_id`. Order id is unpredictable and != registration number.
- `pay-webhook.mjs` -- **signature-verified** (HMAC-SHA256 over `timestamp+rawBody` with the
  Cashfree client secret), success-only, **amount/currency matched**, **idempotent** activation.
- `verify.mjs` -- enumeration-resistant, rate-limited; uniform 404; never leaks names/message.
- `account.mjs` -- lists the caller's dedications using the **verified JWT** user id (no IDOR).
- `admin.mjs` -- allow-list-gated admin ops (metrics/correct/set_status/review); destructive
  actions require `confirm:true`; every mutation audited by the underlying RPC.
- `expire-reservations.mjs` -- scheduled (cron `*/10 * * * *`) reservation expiry.

### Shared library (`netlify/functions/_shared/`)
`response.mjs` (stable error codes) \u00b7 `cors.mjs` (origin allow-list + preflight) \u00b7
`sanitize.mjs` (validation, HTML-escape, reg-no normaliser) \u00b7 `cashfree.mjs` (verified
signature + order create, injectable fetch) \u00b7 `supabase.mjs` (service/user clients, lazy import)
\u00b7 `auth.mjs` (bearer + admin allow-list) \u00b7 `ratelimit.mjs` (Postgres-backed + in-memory).

### Database (`supabase/migrations/`)
- `0007_rate_limit_and_admin.sql` -- `rate_limits` + atomic `tsdr_rate_limit_hit`; `tsdr_verify`
  (safe view); `tsdr_account_dedications`; admin RPCs (`correct`, `set_status`, `review`,
  `metrics`) -- all `SECURITY DEFINER`, EXECUTE-revoked from anon/authenticated, and audited.
- `0008_payment_rpcs.sql` -- `tsdr_get_pending_for_payment`, `tsdr_attach_order`,
  `tsdr_record_payment_event`, and `tsdr_activate_by_order` (amount/currency match -> idempotent
  `tsdr_activate_paid_dedication`).

### Browser flow (`public/`)
- `dedicate.html` + `js/dedicate.js` -- full 4-step journey (occasion/star -> details -> review ->
  pay) calling reserve -> draft -> pay-init, then Cashfree checkout via `payment_session_id`.
- `account/` + `js/account.js` -- Supabase magic-link auth; lists your dedications.
- `admin/` + `js/admin.js` -- admin sign-in, metrics, review/status actions.
- `netlify.toml` -- scheduled function, raw-body note for webhook, CSP extended for Cashfree +
  Supabase; `package.json` adds `@supabase/supabase-js` + test scripts; `.env.example` updated.

## Why it changed
Turns Run 1's data model into working behaviour with the security gates the handover requires
(atomic allocation, verified/idempotent payments, enumeration-resistant verification,
least-privilege admin, audited mutations) -- while keeping the browser free of secrets.

## Grounding (external APIs verified, not guessed)
- **Cashfree webhook:** `Base64(HMAC-SHA256(x-webhook-timestamp + rawBody, CLIENT_SECRET))`
  compared to `x-webhook-signature`; the correct key is the PG **client secret**. Verified
  against Cashfree docs + community reports (Sep 2026).
- **Cashfree create order:** `POST {base}/pg/orders` with `x-client-id`/`x-client-secret`/
  `x-api-version`, returns `payment_session_id`.

## What was actually RUN (in this environment)
```
$ node scripts/test/test_functions.mjs        -> 36 passed, 0 failed
$ node scripts/test_allocation_logic.mjs       -> 20 passed, 0 failed   (Run 1, still green)
$ node -e "import every function + shared module"  -> ALL MODULES IMPORTED CLEANLY
```

## What was actually TESTED (real, executed)
Handler-level, against a mock DB that reproduces the RPC contract + the **real** shared modules:
- **reserve:** method guard \u00b7 missing field \u00b7 success \u00b7 pool-exhausted 409 \u00b7 rate limit (30/10m).
- **draft:** wrong reservation token -> 400 (IDOR blocked) \u00b7 invalid occasion -> 400 \u00b7 success.
- **pay-init:** uses **server** price (client cannot set amount) \u00b7 returns session \u00b7 order id
  unpredictable and != session.
- **pay-webhook:** invalid signature -> 401 \u00b7 valid -> activates + reg number \u00b7 duplicate ->
  idempotent (same number) \u00b7 **amount tampering -> 400** \u00b7 FAILED payment acknowledged, not
  activated.
- **verify:** bad format -> 400 (no DB) \u00b7 nonexistent -> uniform 404 \u00b7 existing -> 200 \u00b7 **never
  leaks names/message** \u00b7 rate limit (15/5m) enumeration resistance.
- **account:** no token -> 401 \u00b7 valid token -> 200 list.
- **admin:** non-admin -> 403 \u00b7 metrics -> 200 \u00b7 destructive without confirm -> 400 \u00b7 with confirm
  -> 200 \u00b7 cannot "correct" the star (immutable) -> 400.
- **units:** XSS escaped \u00b7 length limits \u00b7 reg-no normaliser (spacing/case) \u00b7 **signature accept/
  reject/tamper** \u00b7 disallowed CORS origin -> `ACAO: null`.

## HONEST limitations -- what was NOT executed here
- **No PostgreSQL/Supabase and no network** in this environment, so:
  - the `.sql` (0007/0008) was **not** executed against a real database;
  - the tests use a **mock** that mirrors the RPC contract -- a live Supabase run is still
    required (steps in `docs/INTEGRATION-TESTING.md`).
- **No live Cashfree call** was made; `createOrder` was mocked. Only the **signature algorithm**
  ran for real. A sandbox order + real webhook must be tested with your keys.
- Supabase **JWT verification** is stubbed in tests via an injected verifier; the production
  path calls `supabase.auth.getUser(jwt)`.
- Email magic-link, R2, certificate/map/reveal are **not** in Run 2 (Run 3).

## What remains (next runs)
- **Run 3:** certificate (PDF/A4/share + QR), data-driven star map, cinematic reveal (modes),
  interactive viewer, privacy-mode UIs, media upload (R2 + signed URLs).
- **Run 4:** SEO/a11y/perf completion, nonce-based CSP, automated + true end-to-end tests
  against live Supabase/Cashfree sandbox, backups, deployment/ops docs.

## What depends on live credentials/services
Supabase project (URL, anon, **service-role**) \u00b7 Cashfree app id + **secret** (+ sandbox) \u00b7
webhook reachable at `/.netlify/functions/pay-webhook` \u00b7 admin emails in
`TSDR_ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS` \u00b7 public `window.TSDR_SUPABASE_URL/ANON_KEY` for account/admin pages.

# Run 3 Report -- The Star Dedication Registry

**Scope (per Section 27 Run 3):** certificate generation \u00b7 star map \u00b7 cinematic reveal \u00b7
interactive exploration \u00b7 privacy modes \u00b7 media handling. *No ZIP generated (per Section 25/Section 27).*

---

## What changed (this run)

### Libraries (`lib/`) -- original, dependency-light
- `qr.mjs` -- **self-contained QR encoder** (byte mode, ECC L/M/Q/H, versions 1-10, all 8
  masks, Reed-Solomon over GF(256), format/version BCH). No third-party QR dependency.
- `qr_decode.mjs` -- companion spec decoder used **only** for round-trip self-verification.
- `starmap.mjs` -- data-driven star map via **gnomonic projection** of real J2000 coordinates;
  SVG output; magnitude-scaled dots; honest "artistic rendering -- not a photograph" label;
  HYG attribution. Coordinate validation built in.
- `certificate.mjs` -- premium **A4 PDF** (pdf-lib): original seal, symbolic + scientific
  details, embedded QR + star map, authenticity hash, mandatory disclosure, WinAnsi-safe text
  sanitiser so any user input renders without crashing. Signature is an authorised role only.

### Netlify Functions (`netlify/functions/`)
- `reveal.mjs` -- privacy-filtered reveal payload: **public by rn**, **private/unlisted only by
  high-entropy token** (rn never unlocks private). Rate-limited; supplies viewer neighbours.
- `download.mjs` -- authorised, **short-lived signed R2 GET** for certificate/star-map; private
  records require the token.
- `media-upload.mjs` -- ownership-checked, type/size-validated **signed R2 PUT** (direct-to-R2;
  no bytes through the function).
- `generate-certificate.mjs` -- internal-secret-gated server pipeline: build map->PNG->R2, QR,
  certificate PDF->R2, record `certificate_versions` + attach keys.
- `_shared/r2.mjs` (query presign GET/PUT) and `_shared/r2put.mjs` (authenticated PUT with
  payload hash) -- AWS SigV4, pure Node crypto, no AWS SDK.

### Browser (`public/`)
- `reveal.html` + `js/reveal.js` + `css/reveal.css` -- original cinematic sequence (sender line ->
  starfield resolves -> navigate -> star distinguishes -> name -> dedication -> details -> actions),
  **sound OFF by default** (WebAudio only on user action), **Skip**, **reduced-motion** path,
  accessible text alternative, and six **modes** (celebration/romance/friendship/memorial/
  newborn/achievement) with memorial restrained.
- `js/viewer.js` -- original interactive **Canvas** sky viewer: highlights the allocated star,
  pan-free zoom, constellation/coordinates/scientific id, honest visibility guidance, Esc-close.

### Database (`supabase/migrations/`)
- `0009_reveal_media_artifacts.sql` -- `reveal_mode`; `tsdr_reveal_public` / `tsdr_reveal_by_token`;
  `tsdr_star_neighbors`; `tsdr_media_register`; `tsdr_artifact_key`; `tsdr_record_certificate`.
- `0010_cert_source.sql` -- `tsdr_cert_source`, `tsdr_attach_starmap`.
  All SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE-revoked from anon/authenticated.

## Why it changed
Delivers the emotional core of the product (the reveal + certificate + explorable star) on top
of Runs 1-2, while holding the honesty and privacy lines: private records never leak via rn,
artefacts are served only through short-lived signed URLs, and the star map/certificate never
imply ownership or scientific naming.

## What was actually RUN + TESTED (executed here)
```
scripts/test_allocation_logic.mjs        20 passed, 0 failed   (Run 1)
scripts/test/test_functions.mjs          36 passed, 0 failed   (Run 2)
scripts/test/test_qr.mjs                 85 passed, 0 failed   (QR)
scripts/test/test_run3_functions.mjs     22 passed, 0 failed   (reveal/download/media/R2)
scripts/test/test_artifacts.mjs          14 passed, 0 failed   (real PDF + star map)

                                       ---------------------
                                        185 passed, 0 failed
```
Highlights of what these prove **for real**:
- **QR:** RS syndromes zero across versions 2-10 & all ECC levels; internal encode->decode
  round-trips exact URLs; single-bit tamper flips syndromes (negative control).
- **Star map:** allocated star projects to the exact chart centre; all neighbours in-bounds;
  SVG rasterises to a 900x900 PNG (a real PNG file is written to `scripts/test/_artifacts/`).
- **Certificate:** a real **~88 KB A4 PDF** is generated, re-parsed with **pdfjs-dist**
  (1 page; registration number, honouree, and the disclosure text all present); the embedded
  QR decodes back to the verify URL.
- **Reveal/privacy:** public-by-rn works; **private-by-rn returns 404** (no leak); token
  unlocks; wrong token 404; occasion->mode mapping (memorial, romance, etc.).
- **Downloads/media:** signed R2 URLs only; private artefacts need the token; media rejects bad
  MIME/oversize and non-owners; object keys namespaced per dedication.
- **R2 SigV4:** endpoint/bucket/params/64-hex signature; nested key paths preserved.
- **Full server pipeline:** generate-certificate builds map+QR+PDF, "uploads" (mock R2), and
  records a certificate version -- 200 ok, gated by internal secret.

A visual QA render of the star map is included at `scripts/test/_artifacts/starmap.png`.

## HONEST limitations -- what was NOT executed here
- **QR was not scanned by a physical device.** Correctness is evidenced by RS syndromes = 0 and

  - migrations `0009/0010` were **not** run against a real DB (mock mirrors the RPCs);
  - **no real R2 upload/download** happened -- `putObject` was mocked; the SigV4 signing code is
    unit-tested for structure/determinism but must be confirmed against a live R2 bucket;
  - the reveal/viewer pages ship with a small demo payload so they render without the backend.

  import will make maps dense. Projection maths is validated regardless.
- Reveal ambient audio is a gentle WebAudio tone (no external asset), started only on user tap.

# Run 4 Report -- The Star Dedication Registry

**Scope (per Section 27 Run 4):** SEO \u00b7 accessibility \u00b7 performance \u00b7 security hardening (strict CSP) \u00b7
automated testing \u00b7 full end-to-end audit \u00b7 deployment & operational documentation. *No ZIP
generated automatically (per Section 25/Section 27) -- produced only on explicit request.*

---

## What changed (this run)

### Security hardening -- strict CSP (no `unsafe-inline`)
- New build tool `scripts/build_csp.mjs`: scans every `public/**.html`, **fails the build** if any
  inline `style=""` or `<style>` remains, **hashes** every inline `<script>` (JSON-LD), and emits
  `public/_headers` with a strict CSP + caching + security headers.
- Refactored **all** pages to remove inline styles (added a utility-class layer to `tsdr.css`);
  externalised the reveal demo payload (`js/reveal-demo.js`) and the dedicate `<style>` block.
- `netlify.toml` now runs `build_csp.mjs` as the build command and defers headers to `_headers`.
- Result CSP: `script-src 'self' <cashfree> <jsdelivr> <4 sha256 hashes>`, `style-src 'self'`,
  `object-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, HSTS, nosniff, Referrer/Permissions-Policy.

### Complete public pages (Section 19)
Added: `how-it-works`, `explore` (+`js/explore.js`, labelled demos), `about`, `faqs` (FAQ JSON-LD),
`contact`, `accessibility`, and legal `standards`, `data-sources`, `privacy`, `terms`, `refunds`,
`content-policy`. Every page has unique title/description/canonical/OG and one `<h1>`.
New function `explore-public.mjs` + `tsdr_explore_public` RPC (public-safe listing).

### Automated audits (runnable, CI-ready)
- `scripts/a11y_audit.mjs` -- lang, headings, alt, labels, skip links, canvas, discernible names.
- `scripts/seo_audit.mjs` -- title/description/canonical/OG, single h1, sitemap membership, robots.
- `scripts/perf_audit.mjs` -- per-page critical weight budget, render-blocking scripts, unused
  preconnects, asset sizes.
- Wired into `package.json`: `npm run audit` (csp+a11y+seo+perf) and `npm run verify:all`.

### End-to-end test
- `scripts/test/test_e2e.mjs` runs the **entire journey** through real function cores + real libs:
  reserve -> draft -> pay-init -> pay-webhook(activate, idempotent) -> verify -> reveal ->
  generate-certificate (real PDF/QR/map) -> download, plus a negative path proving private records
  stay private end-to-end.

### SEO/perf polish
- Rewrote sitemap with all indexable pages; robots already disallows account/admin/private.
- Removed unused `fonts.gstatic.com` preconnect (system font stacks) -> CSP `font-src 'self'`.
- Added public runtime config hook `js/config.example.js` (anon key only; real `config.js`
  gitignored) wired into account/admin/dedicate.

### Documentation set (Section 25)
`DEPLOYMENT.md`, `OPERATIONS-MANUAL.md`, `ADMIN-GUIDE.md`, `SUPPORT-GUIDE.md`,
`BACKUPS-AND-DR.md`, `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md`, `PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`, `KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md`,
this report, and `FILES-MANIFEST-RUN4.md`.

## A real bug caught by the E2E audit (and fixed)
The end-to-end test exposed a **store-time HTML-escaping** bug: `validateDedication` was
`escapeHtml()`-ing values at storage, but every renderer already escapes at **output**
(`textContent` / `esc()` / PDF `safe()`), so "Ada's Light" would display as `Ada&#39;s Light`.
Fixed to the correct **store-raw / escape-on-output** pattern (SQL injection is prevented by
parameterised RPCs; all HTML sinks escape). Re-verified: E2E 17/17, functions 36/36 (no regress).

qr           85 OK                       SEO         0 errors, 0 warnings
run3         22 OK                       PERF        0 errors, 0 warnings (pages 15-28 KB)
cert          8 OK
e2e          17 OK
artifacts    14 OK
----------------
TOTAL       202 passed, 0 failed
```

## HONEST limitations -- what was NOT executed here
- No live Supabase/Cashfree/R2/network in this environment, so migrations/RLS, Cashfree HTTP, and
  R2 upload/download were **not** run live (mocks mirror the contracts). Live steps are scripted
  in `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` and gated by `PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`.
- **Lighthouse** wasn't run (no headless browser); instead a static perf budget audit confirms
  15-28 KB critical weight, deferred scripts, no render-blocking, no unused preconnects. Run
  Lighthouse against the deploy for the official score.
- **QR** is RS-verified + decoder round-tripped but **not phone-scanned** (documented).
- Full details in `KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md`.

## What actually happens on the DEPLOYED free path (what you will click)

1. `/dedicate.html` -> choose occasion + star -> **enter your email (required)** + optional
   phone -> personalise -> Review -> enter promo `TESTFREE100`.
2. `pay-init` redeems the 100% promo server-side, activates the dedication, returns the
   registration number. The browser is redirected to
   `/reveal.html?rn=<REG>&t=<keepsake-token>`.
3. The **reveal** plays and shows the real dedication (name, honouree, message, star facts,
   RA/Dec, magnitude, registration number). "Certificate" button ->
   `/certificate.html?rn=<REG>&t=<token>`.
4. The **certificate** renders (HTML/CSS), with a verification QR and a "Print / Save as PDF"
   button. `/verify.html?rn=<REG>` shows the full public-safe record.
5. In parallel, the browser calls `send-keepsake`. If `EMAIL_API_KEY` is set, Resend emails
   the buyer the three links; if not, the reveal/keepsake still work and the API returns the
   links for on-screen display (we never claim an email was sent).

Private records work too: the **keepsake token** (`?t=`) unlocks the reveal + certificate for
any privacy level, which is exactly why the earlier "blank reveal then home" is gone -- the
private test record now resolves via the token instead of 404-ing on the public-only path.

---

## The keepsake token (design note, so it is never re-litigated)

At **draft** time, `draft.mjs` generates a 32-byte token; only its SHA-256 hash is stored
(reusing the existing `private_access_token_hash` column). The raw token is returned once to
the browser and travels on `?t=` links. The existing `tsdr_reveal_by_token` already gates on
`registration_status='active'`, so the token does nothing until the dedication is paid/free-
activated. Buyer email/phone are **never** exposed to the browser, reveal, verify, or the
certificate -- only `send-keepsake` reads the email server-side to deliver.

---

## HONEST limits (up front, per your rules)

- **Resend**: only the algorithmic path (configured vs not) ran here; the real POST to
  `api.resend.com` needs `EMAIL_API_KEY` + `EMAIL_FROM` on the site. Set them and delivery is live.
- **Cashfree**: unchanged this run except graceful-absent handling; live cards are Run D.
- **R2 PDF**: not in Run A by design -- the certificate is HTML/CSS + `window.print()`. Run B
  wires the R2 PDF + downloads.
- **Supabase**: `0012` must be applied by you (steps below). The tests use the mock contract;
  the SQL is structurally consistent with 0001-0011 (same SECURITY DEFINER + revoke pattern).
- **Physical QR scan**: the encoder round-trips in code; a real phone scan of the printed
  certificate remains a pre-launch check (as with the Run B PDF QR).

---

## Apply steps (in order)

1. **Supabase SQL editor**: run `supabase/migrations/0012_buyer_contact_and_price.sql`
   (after 0001-0011 are already applied). It is additive + idempotent.
2. **Netlify env (optional but recommended for delivery)**: set `EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend`,
   `EMAIL_API_KEY=<your Resend key>`, `EMAIL_FROM="The Star Dedication Registry <no-reply@yourdomain>"`.
   Without these, everything still works; emails just fall back to on-screen links.
3. **Deploy** (GitHub -> Netlify, publish dir `public/`). The build runs `build_csp.mjs` and
   regenerates a clean `public/_headers`.
4. **Click it**: dedicate with `TESTFREE100`, confirm reveal + certificate + verify, then set
   a new price in `/admin/` (Pricing card) and confirm checkout reflects it.

_All shipped files are pure ASCII + HTML entities (no mojibake)._

# Run B Report -- R2 PDF Certificate + Star Map + Signed Downloads (TSDR)

Scope (from your handover, Section 6 "Run B"): R2 PDF certificate + star-map
generation; downloads via signed URLs. Built on the **v5 + Run A** working copy
(no fresh master uploaded this run, which is correct since Run A is not deployed yet).

Key design choice, stated plainly: I did **not** put PDF/sharp work on the payment
path (it would add cold-start + latency and risk to activation). Instead generation
is **on-demand and idempotent** -- triggered when the certificate page is viewed,
run at most once per dedication, and **skipped gracefully when R2 is not configured**
so Run A's printable HTML certificate remains the working fallback. The moment you
add R2 keys, the same buttons produce real PDF/PNG downloads with zero code change.

---

## Definition of "Done" -- item by item (RUN vs DEPENDS)

"RUN" = executed here against the real function cores + the **real** `lib/*`
renderers with **real pdf-lib + sharp** (both confirmed available), over the mock
DB contract. R2 upload is captured in-memory so we can assert on the actual bytes.

| Item | Status | Evidence (what I actually ran) |
|---|---|---|
| Certificate **PDF** generates (real bytes, not a stub) | RUN | `test:runB` step 2: `%PDF` header + **119,366-byte** PDF produced by `lib/certificate.mjs` via `issueArtifacts`. |
| **Star map PNG** generates | RUN | Same step: real `PNG` header asserted (SVG -> sharp). |
| Certificate + map **stored to R2** | RUN (captured) / DEPENDS on keys | `putObject` is exercised with the real bytes + keys `certificates/<rn>.pdf`, `starmaps/<rn>.png`; the actual network PUT uses your `R2_*` keys. |
| **Downloads via signed URLs** | RUN | `test:runB` step 6: `download` returns a real SigV4 `X-Amz-Signature=` URL, `expires_in:300`. |
| Generation is **idempotent** (never regenerates) | RUN | step 3: first call `generated:true`; second call `already:true` with the heavy path **not** invoked. |
| **Graceful when R2 absent** (no crash; HTML cert stays) | RUN | step 1: `issueArtifacts` returns `{skipped:true}` (no throw). step 4: `issue-artifacts` returns `ok:false, TSDR_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE`. |
| **Privacy preserved** for downloads | RUN | step 5: a private record cannot be targeted by registration number alone (404); it requires the keepsake token. |
| Buyer can actually get the file on the deployed site | DEPENDS on your R2 keys | With R2 set: "Download PDF certificate" + "Download star map" appear on `/certificate.html` and open signed URLs. Without R2: the page keeps **Print / Save as PDF** (Run A) and shows an honest note. |

Full suite after Run B (executed here):

--------------------------------------------  TOTAL 238 passed, 0 failed

```

## What happens on the deployed site

**With R2 configured** (`R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_BUCKET`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`,
`R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`):
1. Buyer opens `/certificate.html?rn=<REG>&t=<token>` (from the reveal button or the
   Run A keepsake email).
2. The page renders the HTML certificate, then calls `issue-artifacts` once. On first
   view it generates the PDF + star-map PNG and stores them to R2; later views short-
   circuit (`already:true`).
3. "Download PDF certificate" and "Download star map" buttons appear and open short-
   lived (5-minute) signed R2 URLs via `/download`.

**Without R2** (your current state): steps 1 works as in Run A; `issue-artifacts`
returns `TSDR_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE`; the page keeps **Print / Save as PDF** and shows:
"a downloadable PDF and star map become available once storage is enabled."

Privacy: public records can be prepared/downloaded by registration number; private/
unlisted require the keepsake token (`?t=`), enforced in `tsdr_artifacts_target`
(0013) and `tsdr_artifact_key` (0009) with the same rule.

---

## How the pieces fit (no payment-path changes)

- `_shared/artifacts.mjs` -- `issueArtifacts()` (reusable generator; `r2Configured()`
  guard; returns `{skipped}` when R2 is off) + `realArtifactDeps()` (lazy pdf-lib/sharp).
- `issue-artifacts.mjs` -- buyer-facing, authorised (token or public rn), rate-limited,
  idempotent trigger. Resolves the dedication via **`tsdr_artifacts_target`** (0013).
- `generate-certificate.mjs` -- refactored to call the **same** `issueArtifacts`; still
  gated by `X-TSDR-Internal` for admin/manual regeneration.
- `download.mjs` -- unchanged (already returns signed URLs); now actually reachable
  because artifacts get produced.
- Front-end `certificate.js` -- ensures artifacts on view and wires the two download
  buttons; falls back silently to the HTML print path.

---

## HONEST limits (up front)

- **R2 network PUT/GET** is not exercised against live Cloudflare here (no keys in this
  environment). The **bytes, keys, signing math, authz, and idempotency are all real**;
  only the socket to R2 depends on your credentials.
- Generation runs **on first certificate view**, so the first viewer waits ~1-2s while
  the PDF builds (subsequent views are instant). This is deliberate to keep the payment
  path fast. If you'd prefer pre-generation at activation, that's a small follow-up.
- **Cashfree live cards** remain Run D; **email actual send** remains Resend-key-gated
  (Run A). Nothing here changes those.

---

## Apply steps (in order)

1. **Supabase**: run `supabase/migrations/0013_artifacts_target.sql` (after 0001-0012).
   Additive; one new SECURITY DEFINER function.
2. **Netlify env (to enable downloads)**: set `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_BUCKET`,
   `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` (and optionally `R2_PUBLIC_BASE`). Without
   these, everything still works via the HTML certificate.
3. **Deploy** (GitHub -> Netlify, publish dir `public/`).
4. **Click it**: dedicate with `TESTFREE100` -> reveal -> Certificate. With R2 set,
   confirm both downloads open; without R2, confirm Print / Save as PDF + the honest note.

---

## Why the account page needed a tiny migration (0014)

Your default privacy is **private**, and by design the raw keepsake token is **never stored**
(only its SHA-256 hash -- Run A). So a signed-in owner cannot replay/re-download a **private**
record by registration number through the token/public paths. Run C adds one additive,
SECURITY DEFINER resolver, `tsdr_owner_record(p_user, p_regno)`, that returns the record + its
artifact keys **only when `purchaser_user_id` matches the VERIFIED JWT user**. The Netlify
functions pass the user id from the verified Supabase token, never from the client body, so it
cannot read anyone else's record. `test:runC` step 3 proves a different signed-in user gets 404.

The static reveal page stays **auth-free**: the signed-in dashboard fetches the owner payload
with its JWT and hands it to `/reveal.html?replay=1` via `sessionStorage`, so no auth or extra
scripts were added to the reveal page.

---

## Two pre-existing bugs fixed while in `reveal.js`

1. **Garbled separators.** The star-facts and registration lines used a literal double-escaped
   `\\u00b0` / `\\u00b7`, which rendered as literal `\u00b7` instead of `.`/`degree`. Fixed to
   single `\u` escapes (exactly the "garbled symbol" class you flagged).
2. **Registration line never appeared.** `#l-reg` was populated but never given the `.show`
   class, so it stayed invisible. It is now part of the sequence (and the new closing line).

---

## HONEST limits (up front, per your rules)

- **Viewer fidelity vs. performance.** This is an original build over our HYG neighbours -- it
  matches the *emotion* (named star resolving, constellation context, RA/Dec, neighbours, how to
  find it, replay), not Stellarium's catalogue depth or full sky simulation. That trade-off is
  the licensing/performance decision already locked in Decision 1, not a shortcut.
- **On-device smoothness** is a real-phone check on your deploy; I verified render logic, DPR/
  point budgets, and the 2D fallback, but not pixels on a specific Android.
- **Account re-download** producing an actual file depends on your **R2 keys** being live (bytes,
  keys, signing math and idempotency are all real here; only the socket to R2 is live-only).
  When R2 is off, the honest note points to Print / Save as PDF.
- **Owner replay of private records** depends on **Supabase Auth** (magic-link) being configured
  so you can sign in, and on **migration 0014** being applied.
- **Resend / Cashfree** are unchanged by Run C (Cashfree live cards remain Run D).

---

## Apply steps (in order)

1. **Supabase SQL editor**: run `supabase/migrations/0014_owner_access.sql` (after 0001-0013).
   Additive; one SECURITY DEFINER function; EXECUTE revoked from anon/authenticated.
2. **Deploy** (GitHub -> Netlify, publish dir `public/`). The build runs `build_csp.mjs` and

# Run D Report -- Launch hardening: Cashfree live path, SEO, a11y, security, full verification

Scope (from your handover, Section 5 "Run D"): Cashfree sandbox wired LIVE end-to-end; SEO /
structured data; accessibility pass; security hardening; and a full, written run-vs-reviewed
verification of both the free path and the Cashfree sandbox path. Built on v6 master + Run C.
Migrations 0012-0014 confirmed applied; Run D adds one additive migration, **0015**.

Honesty key: **RUN** = executed here against the real function cores + real shared modules over
the in-memory DB mirroring the RPC contract (the same harness your suite uses); the Cashfree
**signature algorithm runs for real**, the HTTP call is mocked. **REVIEWED/AUDITED** = verified by
the static CSP/a11y/SEO/perf audits + code review, not by a live browser. **DEPENDS ON YOUR LIVE
SERVICES** = works once the named key/DB exists; I did not fake it.

---

## 1. Cashfree sandbox -- end-to-end

I found and fixed the real gap: the client already launched `cf.checkout(...)`, but the return
URL `/reveal.html?order=<order_id>` had **nothing to resolve it** -- a returning payer would have
hit the blank-reveal fallback. Run D closes the loop without putting any load on the payment path:

- **New `tsdr_order_status(order_reference)`** (migration 0015): public-safe status resolver --
  returns only `registration_status`, `registration_number` (null until activation) and
  `privacy_level`. No names/message/PII/token/coordinates.
- **New `pay-status.mjs`** (GET `?order=`): rate-limited (60/5min, polling-friendly), returns that
  status so the returning browser can route to the reveal.
- **`dedicate.js`**: before redirecting to Cashfree, stores `{order_id, keepsake_token}` in
  `sessionStorage` on our origin (never sent to Cashfree). This is what lets a **private** record
  reopen after the redirect -- the raw keepsake token is deliberately never stored server-side.
- **`reveal.js`**: on `?order=`, polls `pay-status` (webhook may lag) with clear, resumable copy
  ("Confirming your payment... keep this page open"), then opens the reveal by `rn` (public) or the
  stashed token (private), and rewrites the address bar to a meaningful `?rn=&t=` link. If the bank
  is slow it offers a **"Check again"** button -- **never** "clear cache and retry".
- Free promo path (`TESTFREE100`) is untouched and still activates with no Cashfree.

| Item | Status | Evidence (`test:runD`, 19 checks, all executed) |
|---|---|---|
| pay-init creates a Cashfree order (sandbox) | RUN | step 1: returns `payment_session_id`; order id server-generated `TSDRORD-...` |
| Invalid signature rejected | RUN | step 3: tampered signature -> **401** (real HMAC via `_shared/cashfree`) |
| Amount / currency mismatch blocked | RUN | step 4: -> **400** AMOUNT_MISMATCH / CURRENCY_MISMATCH |
| FAILED event recorded, not activated | RUN | step 5: 200 ack, still `pending_payment` |
| SUCCESS webhook activates + assigns reg number | RUN | step 6: `TSDR-2026-XXXXXXXX`, `already_processed:false` |
| Duplicate webhook idempotent | RUN | step 7: same number, `already_processed:true` |
| pay-status pending -> active transition | RUN | steps 2 + 8: pending before, active + rn after |
| pay-status leaks no PII | RUN | step 8: asserts no names/message/PII/token/coords in the response |
| Paid reveal opens by rn in the right mode | RUN | step 9: "Amazing Grace" in `achievement` mode |
| Unknown/missing order handled | RUN | step 10: 404 / 400 |
| Free promo path intact | RUN | step 11: 100% promo still activates without Cashfree |

**DEPENDS ON YOUR LIVE KEYS:** a real sandbox order + real webhook delivery with your

## 4. Security hardening

- **New RPCs least-privilege**: `tsdr_owner_record` (0014) and `tsdr_order_status` (0015) are
  `SECURITY DEFINER` with `EXECUTE` **revoked** from `public/anon/authenticated` -- callable only by
  the trusted service-role functions. Verified in-file.
- **No PII in the return path**: `pay-status` returns only status/rn/privacy; `test:runD` step 8
  asserts no names/message/PII/token/coordinates leak. Reveal/verify/certificate PII rules from
  Runs 1-3 are unchanged.
- **Webhook**: signature verification (real HMAC), success-only activation, amount/currency match,
  and idempotency are all re-proven in `test:runD` (steps 3-7).
- **Headers/CSP** (generated `public/_headers`): strict CSP with no `unsafe-inline` (scripts hashed,
  styles externalised), `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `object-src 'none'`, HSTS, nosniff,
  Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy. `connect-src` allows only self + Cashfree + Supabase;
  `frame-src` allows only Cashfree (needed for checkout). REVIEWED: `Permissions-Policy: payment=()`
  is fine because checkout uses a full-page redirect (`redirectTarget:'_self'`), not the in-page
  Payment Request API.
- **No secrets in the client**: grep of `public/` finds only a *warning comment* in the gitignored
  `config.js`; no service-role/provider/R2 secret is present in any shipped client file.
- REVIEWED (live-only): RLS behaviour, real webhook signature with your secret, and R2 signed
  round-trip remain the live checks in `INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` / `PRE-LAUNCH-CHECKLIST.md`.
